subclasses achieve using <i>types</i>. (The \\\"Design Patterns\\\" book doesn't include this pattern,\\n94:  * so think of it as something like a cross between a Flyweight and a State pattern. Like State, we delegate from\\n96:  * Rule to RuleClass for the specific behavior of that rule (though unlike state, a Rule object\\n97:  * never changes its RuleClass). (The \"Design Patterns\" book doesn't include this pattern,\n94:  * so think of it as something like a cross between a Flyweight and a State pattern. Like Flyweight,\n95:  * we avoid repeatedly storing data that belongs to many instances. Like State, we delegate from\n96:  * Rule to RuleClass for the specific behavior of that rule (though unlike state, a Rule object\n97:  * never changes its RuleClass). This avoids the need to declare one Java class per class of Rule,\n98:  * yet achieves the same behavior.)\n99:  *\n100:  * <p>The use of a metaclass also allows us to compute a mapping from Attributes to small integers\n101:  * and share this between all rules of the same metaclass. This means we can save the attribute\n102:  * dictionary for each rule instance using an array, which is much more compact than a hashtable.\n103:  *\n104:  * <p>Rule classes whose names start with \"$\" are considered \"abstract\"; since they are not valid\n105:  * identifiers, they cannot be named in the build language. However, they are useful for grouping\n106:  * related attributes which are inherited.\n107:  *\n108:  * <p>The exact values in this class are important. In particular:\n109:  *\n110:  * <ul>\n111:  *   <li>Changing an attribute from MANDATORY to OPTIONAL creates the potential for null-pointer\n112:  *       exceptions in code that expects a value.\n113:  *   <li>Attributes whose names are preceded by a \"$\" or a \":\" are \"hidden\", and cannot be redefined\n114:  *       in a BUILD file. They are a useful way of adding a special dependency. By convention,\n115:  *       attributes starting with \"$\" are implicit dependencies, and those starting with a \":\" are\n116:  *       late-bound implicit dependencies, i.e. dependencies that can only be resolved when the\n117:  *       configuration is known.\n118:  *   <li>Attributes should not be introduced into the hierarchy higher then necessary.\n119:  *   <li>The 'deps' and 'data' attributes are treated specially by the code that builds the runfiles\n120:  *       tree. All targets appearing in these attributes appears beneath the \".runfiles\" tree; in\n121:  *       addition, \"deps\" may have rule-specific semantics.\n122:  * </ul>\n123:  *\n124:  * TODO(bazel-team): Consider breaking up this class in more manageable subclasses.\n125:  */\n126: // Non-final only for mocking in tests. Do not subclass!\n127: @Immutable\n128: public class RuleClass implements RuleClassData {\n129: \n130:   /** The name attribute, present for all rules at index 0. Also defined for all symbolic macros. */\n131:   public static final Attribute NAME_ATTRIBUTE =\n132:       attr(\"name\", STRING_NO_INTERN)\n133:           .nonconfigurable(\"All rules have a non-customizable \\\"name\\\" attribute\")\n134:           .mandatory()\n135:           .build();\n136: \n137:   /**\n138:    * Maximum attributes per RuleClass. Current value was chosen to be high enough to be considered a\n139:    * non-breaking change for reasonable use. It was also chosen to be low enough to give significant\n140:    * headroom before hitting limits imposed by the compact attribute value storage strategy in\n141:    * {@link Rule}.\n142:    */\n143:   private static final int MAX_ATTRIBUTES = 200;\n144: \n145:   /**\n146:    * Maximum attribute name length. Chosen to accommodate existing and prevent extreme outliers from\n147:    * forming - extreme values create bloat, both in memory usage and various outputs, including but\n148:    * not limited to, query output.\n149:    */\n150:   private static final int MAX_ATTRIBUTE_NAME_LENGTH = 128;\n151: ","url":"https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/github.com/sg-evals/bazel--8.0.0@12f256c9dffb17d03d47b3f06bf34f67c976e4aa/-/blob/src/main/java/com/google/devtools/build/lib/packages/RuleClass.java%3FL1-150"}}}

Configuration Phase\n```java\n// Prepare attribute values\nMap<String, Object> attributeValues = new HashMap<>();\nattributeValues.put(\"name\", \"my_genrule\");\nattributeValues.put(\"outs\", ImmutableList.of(\"output.txt\"));\nattributeValues.put(\"cmd\", \"echo 'hello' > $@\");\n\n// Wrap in typed map\nBuildLangTypedAttributeValuesMap typedAttrs =\n    new BuildLangTypedAttributeValuesMap(attributeValues);\n```\n\n**What happens:**\n- Attributes are collected in a standard Map\n- Map is wrapped in `BuildLangTypedAttributeValuesMap` for type safety\n- Values are stored in build-language types (before native conversion)\n\n### 3. Creation Phase\n```java\nRule rule = RuleFactory.createAndAddRule(\n    pkgBuilder,           // Package being constructed\n    ruleClass,            // Rule class definition (genrule)\n    typedAttrs,           // Attribute values\n    true,                 // failOnUnknownAttributes\n    DUMMY_STACK);         // Call stack for location info\n```\n\n**What happens in RuleFactory.createAndAddRule():**\n\n1. **Validate rule name** (RuleFactory.java:62-69)\n   - Checks \"name\" attribute exists and is a string\n   - Creates a Label from the name\n\n2. **Validate rule placement** (RuleFactory.java:78-85)\n   - Checks workspace-only rules are in WORKSPACE file\n   - Checks build rules are in BUILD files\n\n3. **Process generator attributes** (RuleFactory.java:87-99)\n   - Adds generator_name/generator_function if needed\n   - These track which macro/function created the rule\n\n4. **Call RuleClass.createRule()** (RuleFactory.java:106)\n   - RuleClass defines the rule structure and validates attributes\n   - Converts build-language types to native types\n   - Applies default attribute values\n   - Creates the Rule instance\n\n5. **Add to package** (RuleFactory.java:142)\n   - Rule is added to the package being constructed\n   - Package maintains target lookup by name\n\n### 4. Verification Phase\n```java\n// Verify rule properties\nassertThat(rule.getName()).isEqualTo(\"my_genrule\");\nassertThat(rule.getRuleClass()).isEqualTo(\"genrule\");\nassertThat(rule.containsErrors()).isFalse();\n\n// Access rule from package\nPackage pkg = pkgBuilder.build();\nassertThat(pkg.getTarget(\"my_genrule\")).isSameInstanceAs(rule);\n```\n\n**What can be verified:**\n- Rule metadata (name, class, location)\n- Rule attributes (via `RawAttributeMapper`)\n- Rule containment in package\n- Rule visibility and other settings\n\n## Detailed Test Explanations\n\n### Test: Name Attribute Validation\n\n```java\n@Test\npublic void testMissingNameAttributeProducesError() {\n    // 1. Setup\n    Package.Builder pkgBuilder = newBuilder(...);\n\n    // 2. Config - missing \"name\"\n    Map<String, Object> attributeValues = new HashMap<>();\n    attributeValues.put(\"outs\", ImmutableList.of(\"output.txt\"));\n    attributeValues.put(\"cmd\", \"echo 'hello' > $@\");\n\n    // 3. Attempt creation - should fail\n    RuleFactory.InvalidRuleException e = assertThrows(\n        RuleFactory.InvalidRuleException.class,\n        () -> RuleFactory.createAndAddRule(...));\n\n    // 4. Verify error\n    assertThat(e).hasMessageThat().contains(\"has no 'name' attribute\");\n}\n```\n\n**Code Path:**\n- `RuleFactory.createAndAddRule()` → Line 63: `attributeValues.getAttributeValue(\"name\")`\n- Returns null → Line 64-65: Throws InvalidRuleException\n- Error message: \"genrule rule has no 'name' attribute\"\n\n### Test: Invalid Rule Names\n\n```java\n@Test\npublic void testInvalidRuleNames() {\n    String[] invalidNames = {\"my-rule\", \"my rule\", \"my.rule\"};\n\n    for (String name : invalidNames) {\n        Map<String, Object> attrs = new HashMap<>();\n        attrs.put(\"name\", name);  // Invalid name format\n\n        RuleFactory.InvalidRuleException e = assertThrows(\n            RuleFactory.InvalidRuleException.class,\n            () -> RuleFactory.createAndAddRule(...));\n\n        assertThat(e).hasMessageThat().contains(\"illegal rule name\");\n    }\n}\n```\n\n**Code Path:**\n- `RuleFactory.createAndAddRule()` → Line 69: `String name = (String) nameObject;`\n- Line 71-76: `pkgBuilder.createLabel(name)` → Throws LabelSyntaxException\n- Exception caught and wrapped: \"illegal rule name: my-rule: ...\"\n\n### Test: Attribute Type Validation\n\n```java\n@Test\npublic void testAttributeTypeValidation() throws Exception {\n    Map<String, Object> attributeValues = new HashMap<>();\n    attributeValues.put(\"name\", \"typed_rule\");\n    attributeValues.put(\"cmd\", \"echo hello\");        // String\n    attributeValues.put(\"outs\", ImmutableList.of(...)); // List\n\n    Rule rule = RuleFactory.createAndAddRule(...);\n\n    // Verify types after creation\n    AttributeMap attributes = RawAttributeMapper.of(rule);\n    assertThat(attributes.get(\"cmd\", Type.STRING)).isEqualTo(\"echo hello\");\n    assertThat(attributes.get(\"outs\", BuildType.OUTPUT_LIST)).hasSize(2);\n}\n```\n\n**Code Path:**\n1. Attributes passed to `RuleFactory.createAndAddRule()` as build-language types\n2. `RuleClass.createRule()` converts each attribute using `BuildType.convertFromBuildLangType()`\n3. Conversions:\n   - String → String (no conversion)\n   - ImmutableList → List<Label> (for label lists)\n   - ImmutableList → List<String> (for string lists)\n4. `RawAttributeMapper` allows retrieving attributes with type checking\n\n### Test: Default Attributes\n\n```java\n@Test\npublic void testDefaultAttributeValuesAreApplied() throws Exception {\n    Map<String, Object> attributeValues = new HashMap<>();\n    attributeValues.put(\"name\", \"my_cc_library\");\n    // Don't provide optional attributes\n\n    Rule rule = RuleFactory.createAndAddRule(...);\n\n    AttributeMap attributes = RawAttributeMapper.of(rule);\n    // alwayslink defaults to false\n    assertThat(attributes.get(\"alwayslink\", Type.BOOLEAN)).isFalse();\n    // srcs defaults to empty list\n    assertThat(attributes.get(\"srcs\", BuildType.LABEL_LIST)).isEmpty();\n}\n```\n\n**Code Path:**\n1. `RuleFactory.createAndAddRule()` → `RuleClass.createRule()` → Line 106\n2. `RuleClass.createRule()` iterates through all attributes\n3. For missing optional attributes, `Attribute.getDefaultValue()` is called\n4. Defaults are defined in RuleClass.Builder with `.value()` or computed functions\n5. Example: `attr(\"alwayslink\", BOOLEAN).value(false)` sets default to false\n\n### Test: Workspace Rule Validation\n\n```java\n@Test\npublic void testWorkspaceRuleFailsInBuildFile() {\n    Package.Builder pkgBuilder = newBuilder(\n        PackageIdentifier.createInMainRepo(\"mypkg\"),  // BUILD file context\n        buildFilePath);\n\n    Map<String, Object> attributeValues = new Missing Attribute Error\n- Check: Is the attribute defined in the RuleClass?\n- Check: Is the attribute mandatory or optional?\n- Check: Did you provide the right type?\n\n### 2. Invalid Rule Name\n- Check: Name must match `[a-zA-Z0-9_]+`\n- Check: No spaces, hyphens, or dots\n- Check: Not starting with underscore or digit (optional restriction)\n\n### 3. Type Mismatch\n- Check: BuildType.LABEL_LIST expects List<String> or List<Label>\n- Check: Type.STRING expects String\n- Check: Type.BOOLEAN expects Boolean\n- Solution: Use proper ImmutableList.of(), not raw arrays\n\n### 4. Rule Not in Package\n- Check: Did you call `pkgBuilder.build()`?\n- Check: Did you use `createAndAddRule` not just `createRule`?\n- Check: Is the rule name correct?\n\n## Best Practices\n\n### 1. Use the Test Pattern\n```java\n@Test\npublic void testFeatureName() throws Exception {\n    // Setup\n    Path path = scratch.resolve(\"/workspace/pkg/BUILD\");\n    Package.Builder builder = newBuilder(...);\n\n    // Config\n    Map<String, Object> attrs = new HashMap<>();\n    attrs.put(\"name\", \"test_rule\");\n\n    // Execute\n    Rule rule = RuleFactory.createAndAddRule(...);\n\n    // Verify\n    assertThat(rule.getName()).isEqualTo(\"test_rule\");\n}\n```\n\n### 2. Use Immutable Collections\n```java\n// Good\nattributeValues.put(\"srcs\", ImmutableList.of(\"a.cc\", \"b.cc\"));\n\n// Avoid\nattributeValues.put(\"srcs\", Arrays.asList(\"a.cc\", \"b.cc\"));\n```\n\n### 3. Test Error Cases\n```java\n// Always test both success and error cases\n@Test\npublic void testValidInput() throws Exception { }\n\n@Test\npublic void testInvalidInput() { }\n```\n\n### 4.
